# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document accompanies
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md). It
> expands the RFC's phase sequence into **level-3 tasks that can be committed and verified
> independently**.
>
> The phase order and acceptance gates in the body of RFC-039 are the sole authority. This document
> is responsible only for decomposition to executable granularity and for registering conflicts that
> have not yet been adjudicated.

## Position and Scope

| Level       | Count                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Phases, corresponding one-to-one with RFC-039's phase sequence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **Level 2** | 72 (2026-10-13: 53 +2 = 3.13 / 3.14; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.15; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.16; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17 / 3.18; 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4; 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19 / S7 / S8; 2026-10-18: +2 = 3.21 / S9 (the original registration number for this group was 3.20; it was moved to 3.21 on 2026-10-21, and 3.20 was released for "Existing false positives in dead-code analysis"; see the closure record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); 2026-10-21: +2 = 0.5 / 3.20; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10 (the original S9 number proposed for the 4.4 batch was already used by "Closing out abstraction in the pre-release audit" and was therefore deferred); 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22; 2026-10-25: +1 = 3.23; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11)                                                                              | Task groups, derived from the "Implementation Points" sections of the various documents + the 2026-10-07 audit addendum (3.4 for P3) + the 2026-10-10/11 adjudication supplements (3.5/3.6/3.7/3.8/3.9 for P3) + the 2026-10-12 registration (3.10 for P3) + the 2026-10-12 discovery supplement (3.11 for P3) + the 2026-10-12 second discovery supplement (3.12 for P3) |
| **Level 3** | 193 (2026-10-13: 144 +2 = 3.13.1 / 3.14.1; 2026-10-14: +2 = 3.15.1 / 3.16.1; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17.1 / 3.18.1; 2026-10-16: +2 = 4.6.2 / 4.9.6 (`#450`, user adjudication A); 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4.1; 2026-10-17: +1 = 4.9.7 (`#464`); 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19.1 / S7.1 / S8.1; 2026-10-18: +12 = 4.9.8 / 3.14.2 / 4.10.4 / 4.10.5 / 3.21.1–3.21.4 (formerly 3.20.1–3.20.4, renumbered on 2026-10-21) / S9.1 / S9.2 / S2.2 / 4.4.4; 2026-10-21: +7 = 3.6.2 / 3.6.3 / 3.6.4 / 0.5.1 / 0.5.2 / 3.20.1 / 3.20.2; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10.1; 2026-10-22: +5 = 3.6.5 / 3.6.6 / 4.10.6 / 4.10.7 / 4.10.8; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.11.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 0.5.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22.1; 2026-10-25: +2 = 3.23.1 / 3.18.2; +2 = 3.20.3 / 3.18.3; 2026-10-26: +1 = 3.20.4; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12.1; +1 = 9.12.2; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11.1) | Independently committable actions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

**Unused numbers (not reclaimed)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`. None of the three
has had a corresponding entry since the WBS was established—the history of this document has zero
hits for the three headings `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1`
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can be used for verification),
and there are no references elsewhere in the repository. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of
`05` (test revival and completion): that phase has already been split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and
`8.8`, so P8's numbering is aligned with its phase number (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so
forth).

**Covered by this document**: task decomposition, dependency relationships, parallelization groups,
and binding of acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: the design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) and the argument
for phase ordering (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: newly registered level-2/level-3 rows **must
contain two fields**—"**Owning phase**" (the P phase to which the task truly belongs, which may
differ from the section containing it) and "**Blocks mainline**"; `Pending scheduling` is no longer
the default state, and an unmarked task is deemed **not schedulable**. Numbers must be **unique
across the entire document** (tasks on separate tracks use their track number, such as `029g.x`, and
must not occupy `N.x`). **Checkoff/completion registration is based on "task ID + evidence (gate
values, run ID, issue number, file:line)" and does not record commit hashes**—`pull --rebase`
rewrites local hashes, so hard-coded hashes inevitably become invalid (2026-10-13 example:
`94ad03ce4`→`631eb3e3c`). Use `git log` to look up commit hashes.

## Current State: Gaps Discovered During Decomposition

While consolidating the "Implementation Points" sections of eight documents into the main table,
**five hard gaps in the documentation system itself** were discovered. All have now been closed:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Resolution                                                                                                                                                                    |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps**. RFC defines P5 (checker splitting) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents contains a section describing how to perform the split; `annotations.rs` appears in the directory tree in `01-routing.md` and is the only mention in the entire batch.                                                    | P5's steps are supplied by this document (see below)                                                                                                                          |
| G2  | **All three ownership assignments for the `include!` refactoring exist, but none has been implemented**. The RFC places it in P4; `02` assigns it to itself by reference; `06` also lists it under S7. However, `02`'s change list (6 additions + 20 modifications) **does not contain `semantic_tokens.rs`**.                                                                | The implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 in this document (after P4 is complete); references from the RFC and `06` have been updated to point here                    |
| G3  | **Three coexisting lists of CI scripts**. The `RFC` lists four `scripts/ci/*.py`; `01` lists four scripts + one `cargo test`; `07` lists four other checks under entirely different names; `04` also requires `check-synth-boundary.py`. After deduplication, there are **at least nine new scripts and no unified list**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9. | See §P9 in this document for the unified list (11 scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents reference this table                                                 |
| G4  | **Conflict over the fate of `precedence_inline.rs`**. `06` counts it among the "1,005 lines that must be revived", while B6 in the same document says it "must be deleted together with" the `Precedence` enum; `05` advocates deletion.                                                                                                                                      | **Revive in P1, delete with Pratt in P8** (decision registered as C1)—first restore CI coverage, then delete the file in 8.6 together with the Pratt ladder                   |
| G5  | **Conflicting descriptions of the L2 goal**. The RFC and the index in this directory say "grammar-driven syntax", whereas `05` self-assesses that it "**did not use true grammar-driven parsing (LALRPOP), and only achieved declarative table lookup**".                                                                                                                     | This has been consolidated into **complete LALRPOP grammar-driven parsing** (05 §Syntax: complete grammar-driven parsing); the G7/G8 acceptance gates enforce that commitment |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0  仓库维护机制与代码放置规程     08        ← 新增，先立规矩
P1  复活孤儿测试并修缺陷           06 §S1
P2  建立等价性判据基线             07 全部
P3  修正确性漏洞（最小方案）        02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4  阶段契约与统一 Driver          02
P5  checker 文件内拆分             02 + 本文补齐
P6  类型表示单一化                 03
P7  中间表示 SSA 化                04      } 可并行
P8  前端范式变更                   05      }
P9  防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单）     01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正             06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: the three prohibitions (no fabrication, no endless filling, and no
patching when a refactor is required) constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be
established before work begins; otherwise, while reviving tests in P1, work would already begin
adding `mod` declarations to `tests/mod.rs`—precisely the cause of the G4 conflict.

The fix = reduce `stderr` to `diagnostics: [[code, file, line], …]` + retain only **crash marker lines** (panic / ICE / backtrace header), adding `strip_ansi` (the run side includes color codes) and position lookahead (the position can still be found when source snippets appear between the diagnostic header and `-->`); `exit` / `stdout` / `check.*` / `expectation` remain unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | 0.5.1         | Regenerate the 356-line baseline; `check-corpus-parity` has an empty diff; **rerunning the probe after changing locale text still yields zero diff** (the rationale is sound and has been measured); mutation check: changing a `run.exit` to 99 reports one difference; cost = the corpus layer no longer verifies runtime text, which is instead covered by locale unit tests / `yx explain` snapshots / generated-artifact reconciliation; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks mainline: No**                                                               |
|                                                            | 0.5.2 Channel wiring and calibration: the pre-commit `cargo test` hook and CI switch to the `fast` (before local commit) and `full` (before push and CI baseline) dual channels; the 2600-tier resource profile is calibrated by measurement (currently extrapolated); `test-specification.md` gains a detailed section on resource markers and exclusive tiers (**pending scheduling**; follow-up slice of #486)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | 0.5.1         | The pre-commit `cargo test` hook no longer runs the full suite bare (currently `cargo test --all` runs everything; the "Common Commands" section of AGENTS.md specifies the fast/full dual channels and the red line against running the full `--ignored` suite bare); CI and the pre-commit local path use the same channel policy; the 2600-tier marker's peak and elapsed time are measured values rather than extrapolated values; `test-specification.md` contains the corresponding detailed section; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks mainline: No** |

> **There is no size-baseline task.** The 2026-10-03 decision canceled all line-count / size gates;
> scale problems are addressed through separation of responsibilities (see Prohibition 2, criterion
> A in `08`, which requires human judgment). **0.2.1 is this phase's core acceptance criterion**: if
> the gate cannot report the known parallel representations, the criterion design is wrong and the
> gate is false.

### P3 Fix Correctness Defects (Minimal Approach)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, vulnerability-fix half + **the
2026-10-07 silent-drop-point audit (3.4)**

> **Routing of semantic corrections (D59, user adjudication on 2026-10-11)**: if this track or a
> later phase discovers a defect for which "the old behavior does not count as success"—**A silent
> failure / B silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and resource loss** (crashes, stack overflow,
> silently wrong answers)—it **may be fixed alongside the refactoring**, provided all four
> conditions are met: ① a red criterion must exist first and genuinely be red; ② the externally
> observable behavior of programs that "previously compiled successfully and ran correctly" must not
> change (they must not start failing, and their existing diagnostic codes / text / exit codes must
> not be removed or modified); ③ registration in the same commit (this row must identify the defect
> category + include a revision note for the phase + include the "Defect category" line in the PR's
> required block); ④ the baseline must be regenerated in the same commit + the diff must be manually
> reviewed. **The three categories D user-visible semantics / E external contract tables / F
> RFC-level design changes still require prior user adjudication.** See
> [07 §Routing Criteria for Semantic Corrections](07-equivalence-oracle.md) for the expanded
> criteria and A–F whitelist; the approach of "default to a super-refactor and register it
> afterward" is **not adopted** (the rationale is in RFC-039 D59: without a red-first criterion,
> repairs and regressions cannot be distinguished, which also conflicts with "C5′ does not exist"
> and "no relaxation whatsoever").

      in [#509](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/509)); **3.21.3 was completed on
      2026-10-25** (commit `a8ad0530`; criterion chain and residual findings appear below and in
      [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); **3.21.4 was completed on 2026-10-25** (commit `3b6df553`; a
      fourth hand-written documentation contradiction remains in
      [#509](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/509)); **the entire group was archived on
      2026-10-25**: all four level-3 tasks passed verification/review and were pushed, all local
      gates were green, and each batch's CI conclusion was registered according to its coverage
      relationship (execution currently in progress is incorporated into the unified pre-release
      verification; see each issue's closure comments); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks mainline: No**)

>

- [ ] **3.22 Decomposition semantics for function-type annotations** (1 level-3 task, registered on
      2026-10-24, issue [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501); source = F4
      investigation from the round-2 review of batch 4.10; **3.22.1 pending scheduling**—unify the
      decomposition of annotations that return function types so that "a function declared to return
      a function type" has a valid form; **Owning phase: P3; Blocks mainline: No**)

- [ ] **3.23 Silent degradation on manifest parse failure / missing required field** (1 level-3
      task, registered on 2026-10-25, issue
      [#503](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/503); source = measurements during WBS
      4.9.8 implementation—when `yaoxiang.toml` cannot be parsed (invalid TOML) or lacks a required
      field such as `[package] version`, the CLI **does not report a manifest error** and silently
      degrades to single-file semantics ⇒ the cross-file reference pool becomes ineffective ⇒ false
      `W1002`×2 (`a.yx:1:1` / `b.yx:1:1`, with both types referenced by the entry point), while
      `check` still exits 0; pre-fix and post-4.9.8-fix behavior is identical item by item (predates
      #465 and is unrelated to this fix); D59 **category D** (externally observable behavior),
      semantics pending adjudication (A: report an error and exit / B: continue but emit a W code /
      C: retain the current behavior); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks mainline: No**)

> **Batch (user adjudication on 2026-10-14)**: the four small items 3.13 / 3.14 / 3.15 / 3.16 are
> **highly related** (method binding, decision, and inference entry point belong to one path; 3.16
> is another carrier of the same "named argument" criterion—stdlib native exports), and **are merged
> into one team**—the team scope is broadened from "one level-2 task" to "a batch of related small
> items"; the batch composition, rationale, and deviations must be registered when the team closes
> (see the record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)).

**Acceptance**: vulnerability criteria turn green, and **deliberately removing the fix must turn
them red again**. **Risk**: adding E4018 to the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (changing code first is forbidden);
3.4.3 requires manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 are governed by C2/C3 and gate 9.2 in P9. **Risk**: the
new diagnostic in 3.4.1 is a behavioral change, so the corpus differential baseline and CHANGELOG
must be updated in sync.

> **Stop-the-bleeding path (adjudication on 2026-10-05)**: P3 need not wait for all of P2. The only
> hard prerequisites for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red
> criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (the IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, and
> performance baseline) may proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping correctness defects such as the
> silent acceptance of `Sorted(3)` must not be blocked by snapshot-infrastructure work. 3.3
> (panic-to-diagnostic conversion) has no prerequisite and may land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted on 2026-10-02;
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactoring itself; it is a specification-compliance deletion forced by
an ordering constraint.** It is distinct from P10 S5 "reducing the visibility of `pub` items"
(Rust's `pub(crate)` narrowing).

**Why it must be completed before P4 as a whole**: 029g's deletion surface overlaps with the rewrite
surface of four refactoring phases. Reversing the order would mean repeating the same deletions and
baseline updates on the new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and syntax branches                                         | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexer convergence / 8.3 grammar construction / 8.6 Pratt deletion / 8.7 splitting `parse_assign_after_target`)                                                                                                                                                         |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                       | `parser/ast.rs:247/270`                                                                                            | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory rename (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the pub branch of `collect_exports`         | `typecheck/checker.rs:2150/3802/3836`, `environment.rs:284`                                                        | **P5** (the `include!`→true-`mod` conversion and checker split are **pure moves**, and the pub branch will be moved verbatim into the new files)                                                                                                                                   |
| Dead-code exemptions `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs:389-407` (inside `check_project`)                         | **P4 4.2.2** (`check_project` slimming)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Public` modifier in `semantic_tokens`                              | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs:450/498`                                                                     | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `is_pub` ignoring bindings in `ir_gen`                              | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs:1378`                                                                                       | **P7** (`ir_gen.rs` SSA conversion)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| 31 occurrences of `pub` in `src/std/`                               | `std/list.yx` (25) / `json.yx` (4) / `option.yx` (1) / `result.yx` (1)                                             | No phase modifies these directly, but **P6's hard acceptance criterion requires no difference in `src/std/` (`git diff --stat src/std/` is empty; direct-drive tests under `tests/` are an exception, as described in P6's hard-acceptance section)**—they can only land before P6 |

**Additional benefit**: 029g deletes the dead `env.exports` table (populated by production code and
consumed nowhere repository-wide) and the `is_exported`/`is_visible` accessors ⇒ P4's 4.3 obligation
ledger loses one "produced but never consumed" field.

**Acceptance (behavioral changes; red criterion first)**:

1. **Red before green**: each of the two behavioral changes must land a red criterion before code
   changes—① `pub` changes from "silently accepted" to a parse error; ② unreferenced top-level
   bindings in Lib/Script roles newly report W1001 (pub items were previously absolutely exempt).
2. **Test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` cases in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and the `is_pub` fixtures, they must be replaced by
   equivalent new cases.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (new parse errors + new W1001) ⇒ update
   the corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG in the
   same commit; zero diff is expected for IR snapshots, and any non-zero diff requires an
   explanation.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` are all green.

**Ordering**: overlap with 3.4 files (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be serial and
cannot run in parallel; **the whole track must precede P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track does not
depend on 3.4; implementers may choose which to begin based on available context.

> **Commit cross-reference (2026-10-08)**: while this section's incorporation (the P3.5 chapter +
> main-chain/constraint rows) was being added during the parallel-stream workspace cleanup, it was
> included in `97071c81b` in the same commit. That commit's message records "3.4 addendum
> registration + D57/D58 adjudication filing" and does not mention this track. History is not
> rewritten; following the established precedent (`d5b18390`, see the P2 landing note), it is
> registered in place as evidence for post-hoc bisection.

### P4 Phase Contracts and the Unified Driver

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S1–S5. **This phase and P3's fixes must be
two separate commits.**

      `ExplicitSurfaces`, rather than scanning the directory for entry points; the fallback
      `collect_compilation_refs` / `helpers.rs:454` is used when all roots fail); (b) 4.9.2 upgrades
      granularity to `HashMap<ModulePath, HashSet<String>>`, with `collect_ref_pool` as the sole
      assembly entry point (`helpers.rs:429-449`), and the consumer at `arms.rs:603-609`'s
      `other_module_refs` sends the union of all module entries other than the current unit to
      `DeadCodeAnalyzer`, so a module's own names (top-level definitions and local bindings, except
      imported `use` names) no longer exempt a same-named definition in another module; (c) 4.9.3's
      `arms.rs:611-623` `match us.role` assembles the pool only for Bin/Internal (`set_exempt_pub`)
      and Lib (`set_cross_file_refs`), while Script/Test take the `_ => {}` default arm (judging by
      the file-local definition-use graph). **User's merged adjudication on 2026-10-16**: the three
      level-3 tasks are merged into round 1 (the rationale is that all concern the "origin and
      identity of the pool" and that merging avoids repeatedly rewriting the intermediate state
      after `collect_project_refs` is deleted; 4.9.4 / 4.9.5 concern consumption refinement and
      test-pool separation, depend on 4.6.1's `use`-path resolution, and forcing them into the same

value-edge information (the read side `Load{src: Global}` occurs before that slot's first `Store{dst: Global}` and crosses units) enters the help, naming the violated edge and the qualified name of the read slot; | 5.2 Extract `refinement`    | 5.2.1 Move out the refinement block (three noncontiguous regions in the original `checker.rs`; now one file, `checker/refinement.rs`, containing `ReturnRefinement` / `RefinedWalkCtx`), leaving the shared surface and its six cross-boundary shared items in place                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | 5.1.1         | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|                             | 5.2.2 Narrow the refinement entry point to "`&self` + returns diagnostics", **without leaving impl**: `collect_refined_binding_checks` (now `checker/refinement.rs`, `pub(super)`) no longer uses `&mut self` to write implicitly to the error channel; the diagnostic set is returned upward, and its sole write point is the sole caller `check_module_impl` (`checker.rs`, symbol location). **Rationale for remaining in impl**: this family's field surface contains only `self.env` (so leaving impl is technically feasible), but converting "the entry point and the entire walk" to free functions would also move these call sites: `resolve_type_annotation` has 8 production sites (6 annotation-path sites + `refined_walk_stmt` + `collect_refined_var_names_in_stmt`), `collect_param_refinements` has 2 production sites + 1 test site, and it would create a reverse dependency from "shared surface (signature collection)" to refinement—the opposite of this step's goal of eliminating coupling | 5.2.1         | C1 zero-diff; four **symbol criteria**: ① the signature does not contain `&mut self`; ② the function body contains zero `env.errors`; ③ the **returned** diagnostic write point exists only in the caller; ④ the entry point still has 1 call site |
| 5.3 Extract `annotations`   | 5.3.1 Move out the 10-item annotation-validation family (now one file, `checker/annotations.rs`; `check_annotation_type_names` is the `pub(super)` entry point, the other 9 items are private to the family, and `is_predicate_head` is a family-local free function)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 5.2.2         | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|                             | 5.3.2 **Keep `collect_used_in_type` (`pub(crate)`) as `pub(crate)` and under the `checker` module path**: it is a **shared-surface** function (consumed by both `collect_function_signature` and `inference/statements.rs:16`), and 5.3.1 was empirically measured **not to move it** ⇒ the "zero caller changes" criterion **was already satisfied**, so **do not add** a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export. **Constraint retained**: if it is moved with the family in the future (into annotations or another later batch), `checker.rs` must add a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export so that the existing import remains unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 5.3.1         | **Zero caller changes** is this step's hard acceptance criterion: `git diff src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs` is empty                                                                                                          |

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is moved with the annotation family in the future, a
`pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export **must** be added to `checker.rs` so that the import
remains unchanged; 5.3 was empirically measured **not to move it**, so this batch added no re-export
because the criterion was already satisfied.

**Split only proven large blocks.** Do not split modules that are only 200–400 lines—this adds
navigation cost, reduces cohesion, and yields no decoupling benefit. The remaining submodules
(signatures / type_defs / imports) wait until their files grow back to 1,500 lines.

**Hard acceptance criterion (revision as of 2026-10-27, carrying forward the 2026-10-26 adjudication
②)**: `git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be **empty in every round from
6.1 through 6.7**. **Direct-drive test files** under `tests/` (tests that directly drive modified
code, such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) may be modified when mechanically required, but
every change must be registered with **file / line count / rationale**; **tests must not be deleted
and assertions must not be relaxed**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavioral-fix** batch (new
diagnostic code + baseline update), does not qualify for this hard acceptance criterion, and is a
separate round requiring separate adjudication.

> **Revision relationship**: the original criterion (2026-10-26 adjudication ②) was written as
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, treating the entire `tests/` directory as requiring zero
> differences. The revised criterion **only changes direct-drive test files from "zero differences"
> to "may be changed but must register file / line count / rationale"**; the zero-difference
> requirement for the **corpus layer (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and
> standard library (`src/std/`)** remains unchanged. First application = 6.1.2: only
> `tests/formatter_integration.rs` changed by **3 lines** (2 `assert_format_eq` occurrences of
> `i64`→`Int64` + one comment line), with the rationale that the expected type name in the assertion
> must be synchronized with the changed spelling; no tests were deleted and no assertions were
> relaxed. See 09a "P6 6.1 Name-Normalization Closure".

**Risk**: the 8.4 dual-parser differential is an **unskippable equivalence gate**. If it reports
non-equivalence, the problem must be resolved before switching traffic in 8.5, rather than masked
after the switch through C5′. If any of the approximately 89 BP references are missed during the 8.6
migration, associativity will **silently change** (without an error, but with a different parse
result).

> **8.3–8.6 depend on D0/D2 from `08`**: if the grammar file is placed outside `parser/`, or the
> action code refers backward to `sema`, it will be blocked by `check-boundary.py`.

### P9 Anti-regression Gates (Unified Script List — This Document Supplies the Original Gap G3)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-regression Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative list of all CI scripts** (11 scripts). Scripts are connected to CI
at their "introduction phase"; those marked report-only are converted to hard failures in P9
(changing from "report" to "fail"). Existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation
aliases, etc.) must wait until P6/P8 to reach zero; converting them to hard failures early would
leave CI permanently red and cause gates to be manually bypassed. When P9 becomes a hard failure,
the gates should be exactly green. P2's 2.5, P4's 4.3.2, and P7's 7c.1 reference this table and do
not create separate lists.

- [ ] **9.1–9.11: Connect the eleven scripts at their introduction phases and convert them to hard
      failures in P9** (details in the table below)
- [x] **9.12 Preconditions for conversion to hard failures: rebaseline criterion-C wiring points and
      clear the stale inventory** (2 level-3 tasks, **registered and closed on 2026-10-26**): the
      title written at registration, "clear the inventory of same-name multiple definitions", was a
      **mislabel**—violations under `--strict` come only from criterion C (entry wiring), while
      criterion A (same-name definitions, 181 entries) is report-only and never makes `--strict`
      red; this was corrected during closure. Root cause: `STAGE_MARKERS` was calibrated on
      2026-10-06 against the pre-P4 state of "six paths manually wired independently"; its comment
      explicitly states "after P4 unifies the Driver, this table is deleted along with the
      inventory", but after P4 landed, neither the vocabulary nor the 19 `KNOWN_WIRING_SITES` were
      retired. The criterion therefore classified the Driver's **own phase table**
      (`Stage::DeadCodeAnalysis` variant name, `slug` string table, `state.rs` dependency table) as
      a violation, exactly opposite to the rule text of Prohibition 3, criterion C ("adding an Nth
      entry **instead of registering it in the declarative phase table** → violation"). Resolution =
      **rebaseline the criterion**, not add a whitelist. Results: `--strict` violations 9 → 0,
      inventory 19 → **7**, measured wiring points 18 → **8** (7 inventory + 1 dispatch point),
      criterion-A same-name definitions 181 → 180 (the `build_registry` same-name entry was
      deleted), and `pub(crate)` 167 → 166. **Owning phase: P9; Blocks mainline: No (blocks P9's
      conversion to hard failures).**
  - 9.12.1 Rebaseline delivery (four commits, each described in the closure record in
    [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)): ① **rebaseline the criterion**—LEX is merged into `Parsing`
    (`src/driver/stage.rs` and 02 §Target Design 1 both state that `Parsing` = "lexing + parsing
    (lex → parse)"), DEADCODE/MONO recognize only call forms, `#[cfg(test)] mod` uses **brace
    pairing** to determine the exemption range, `dispatch` is listed separately as
    `DECLARED_DISPATCH_POINTS`, and 13 stale inventory entries are cleared (each with a removal
    rationale, empirically divided into three classes: 5 functions deleted / 5 functions still
    present but no longer carrying phase primitives / 3 entries demoted to a single phase solely
    because LEX was merged); ② **derive arm forms**—the 12 variants' `self.<arm>(` forms are derived
    from the exhaustive match in `dispatch` (`Stage::ALL` provides the variants, a single source) +
    three fail-loud guards (variant source missing / arm not found / arm has no `fn` definition),
    and the false positive caused by including the signature line in the measured body (which made
    `fn check_project(` self-match TC) is fixed (`fn_body` cuts off the signature section; the
    measured "≥2 → <2 phases" reduction is 0); ③ **turn coverage declarations into facts**—the phase
    implementations of 4 variants (VendorConsistency / Discovery / Registry / RoleClassification)
    are shared primitives outside the Driver and are truthfully registered as known blind spots; ④
    **describe blind spots by nature**—a live wiring (`collect_package_refs`, called from
    `driver/arms.rs:626`) and a dead-code candidate (`build_registry`, zero callers) cannot receive
    the same disposition. Three independent review rounds all judged `needs_revision` (medium →
    medium → low; all three findings were genuinely fixed), and the fourth round **passed** with no
    findings. **Acceptance**: `python scripts/ci/check-fanout.py --strict` exits 0 (0 violations / 6
    inventory entries / 1 dispatch point) ✅
  - 9.12.2 Expand criterion-C coverage and clear `build_registry` (**completed; closed on
    2026-10-26**; user adjudication **D1-A include + D2-A delete**): ① **delete** the zero-caller
    chain `build_registry` / `build_registry_from` (formerly `orchestrator.rs:379-396`) and the
    now-orphaned `discover` (formerly `:416-418`); `DiscoveredFile` / `Discovery` /
    `extract_module_info` / `parse_file` are all retained; because `build_registry` was exposed
    through `src/lib.rs:27 pub mod frontend` → `frontend/module/mod.rs:20 pub mod orchestrator`, it
    was a public item of the lib target, so deletion required separate adjudication as an external
    API-surface change. ② **Include** phase primitives that still exist outside the Driver after
    deletion: VendorConsistency ← `ensure_vendor_consistency`, Discovery ← `discover_with_used`,
    RoleClassification ← `roles::classify` (narrowed to the qualified form—a bare name would falsely
    match same-named helpers in other modules, as shown by a reproducible construction), and
    Registry ← `extract_module_info` (not the bare `.register(`, which has many same-named
    occurrences in the repository). ③ Exactly one newly exposed item: `collect_package_refs`
    (`driver/helpers.rs:1057`, live code, called from `driver/arms.rs:626`) is registered, bringing
    the inventory 6 → 7 and measured wiring points 7 → 8. ④ The criterion docstring's coverage
    declaration is changed to facts: merge the old "6+6" division into **10+2**, add the R1–R3
    determinable inclusion criteria, delete "anything constructing phase data is not collected",
    which is disproved by five table entries, and correct three stale line numbers / unmeasured
    values and two inaccurate comments (`orchestrator.rs:7` module header, `statements.rs:737`
    reference to the deleted function). Behavior-neutral: 356 corpus lines byte-for-byte identical,
    216 snapshots with zero drift, `cargo test --lib` 2855/0/2, `pub(crate)` 167 → 166 (the
    inevitable result of deleting `discover`). Review chain: round 1 `needs_revision` (1 medium + 2
    low) → fix → round 2 **pass** (independently reproduced with a self-made probe); this was
    followed by the F4/F5/F6/F8 cleanup round → narrow review **pass** (an AST
    statement-by-statement comparison confirmed that the criterion had no incidental changes).
    **Owning phase: P9; Blocks mainline: No.**

> **There is no line-count ratchet script** (`check-file-size.py` + `baseline.toml` were canceled on
> 2026-10-03). Scale problems are addressed through 08's Prohibition 2, criterion A (separation of

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Chain

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 stop-the-bleeding path (adjudication on 2026-10-05)**: P3's prerequisites are narrowed to
> **2.3.2 + 2.4.1 + 2.4.3** (the multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR
> verifier, 2.2 snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential and performance baseline) may proceed in
> parallel with P3. Stopping correctness defects is not blocked by criterion-infrastructure work;
> however, **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified Driver requires all three layers of
> criteria to be in place.

| Constraint                | Reason                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P0 → P1                   | The three prohibitions constrain every action in P1 (1.2.x adds `mod` declarations to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                 |
| P1 → P2                   | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| P2 (after narrowing) → P3 | The vulnerability criteria must **first be written in red** (2.4.1/2.4.3) + the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist, so that the red tests have somewhere to run                                                                                         |
| P3 → P4                   | Fix bugs before refactoring; reversing the order would cause bugs to become fixed into the phase table as "established behavior"                                                                                                                                |
| P3.5 → P4                 | The deletion surface of RFC-029g (#399) covers the rewrite targets of P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8 (the list appears in §P3.5 of this document)—reversing the order means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, new checker layout, and new IR (D56) |
| P4 → P5                   | The `include!` refactoring and the checker split modify the same file                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| P5 → P6                   | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated; otherwise a regression cannot be bisected to a specific location                                                                                                                                        |
| P6 → P7 / P8              | If type representations do not converge first, the new IR will grow into a third representation                                                                                                                                                                 |
| P7/P8 → P9                | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 and the `pub(crate)` leak count in 9.3 reach their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                                                     |

### Parallelization Groups

| Group                                        | Members                                                                            | Basis                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only group authorized by the RFC)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | The file sets are disjoint: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                         |
| B                                            | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Each independently adds a `mod`; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| C                                            | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three layers of criteria are mutually independent                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| D                                            | 4.4 (proof-layer wrap-up) ∥ the entire P6 line                                     | 02 explicitly states in S5 that it "should proceed independently of S1-S4"                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| E                                            | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | All are mutually independent after 4.1 is complete                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| F                                            | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced in P0 may be developed in parallel) | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| G                                            | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | The file sets are disjoint (verified on 2026-10-05: S3 modifies `docs/src/dev/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 modifies `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 modifies two `pub` sites in `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; no pair overlaps) |

**Cannot run in parallel (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (B6/E5 use
the same files in one batch); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (now unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (`checker.rs` /
`environment.rs` are the same files—the external track's pub-deletion batch and the silent-channel
fixes must be serial).

**New internal document constraints**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly serial; 7a→7b→7c→7d
is strictly serial, and the `Phi` arm in 7b must precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is an independent phase rather than part of P9**—gates are "post-hoc checks"; rules are
  "pre-hoc judgment". P9's scripts cannot prevent "patching when a refactor is required"; only the
  D0–D4 review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative list of CI scripts**—the three coexisting lists that constituted
  original gap G3 prevented closure. After consolidation, the 11 scripts each belong to one phase,
  and report-only status and conversion timing are explicitly traceable.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an "independent track to do after the
  refactor"**—its deletion surface overlaps with the rewrite surfaces of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039
  D56). Deferring it until after the refactoring means repeating the same deletions on the new
  grammar, new checker layout, and new IR. This is the same lesson as incorporating `2315`/`2341`
  into 7e and RFC status corrections into S4.
- **P5's steps are supplied by this document rather than incorporated into P4**—P4 is already the
  largest single-point risk, and adding the checker split would exceed the rollback granularity.
- **5.3.2 uses "zero caller changes" as hard acceptance**—the split must be independently
  revertible.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says "the fixed defect must
  not be reverted" and lacks a requirement to record what was fixed. **Execution convention**: every
  1.3.x fix's commit message must cite the name of the fixed test and the defect description as
  evidence for post-hoc bisection.
- ~~**7b.1 granularity is too coarse**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: split into two exclusive commits,
  7b.1a / 7b.1b, under DoD 6.
- **8.2.1 granularity is too coarse**—approximately 89 renamed references; a missed change
  **silently changes associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding level-3 tasks**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: D17 landed as 7e.1,
  and D20 landed as 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34 opcode reclamation was incorporated into 7e.1, with
  deletion deferred to P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally assigned to an "independent issue"**~~
  **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core
  language semantics and must not be left for future implementation.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()`) has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**:
  incorporated into P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode-gate extractor) has ambiguous ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated
  into the generation-time gate task in P10 S6.

## Conflict Registration: All Adjudicated

> **The original "Conflict Registration" C1–C8 and the open questions in the various documents have
> all converged into the
> [RFC-039 decision registry](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) (D1–D50).** No
> unresolved item remains in this document. The one-to-one mapping is:

| Original number | Issue                                                                               | Decision                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| C1              | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8                           | **Revive it** (P1's premise is that revival provides tests with real assertions); P8's 8.6 deletes the file together with Pratt                                            |
| C2              | The "grammar-driven" commitment vs table-driven implementation                      | **Fully implemented**: LALRPOP is adopted; see "Syntax: complete grammar-driven parsing" in `05`                                                                           |
| C3              | Conflict between the C6 category definition and the S1 classification               | **S1 is classified under C6** (revival requires only regression tests, not equivalence criteria); the C6 definition is expanded to include "pure deletion or pure revival" |
| C4              | `test_release_plan_spans_consumed` whitelist                                        | **No whitelist**; the set difference must be empty. If it cannot be empty, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective → change it to `PlanId` (D20/D41)                       |
| C5              | Internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions have not been verified | **P4's 4.2.7 lists this as a mandatory verification item**; verify it line by line during implementation                                                                   |
| C6              | The wasm playground fix requires P4's `ProgramKind`                                 | **The wasm-path defect fix is incorporated into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                                                                     |
| C7              | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                                  | **Assigned to 04** (P7 batch c); see D21                                                                                                                                   |
| C8              | `upvalue_count` correction and the `.42` version number                             | **Fix it + change `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the format header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`); see D17                                 |
